Carries the meaning.\",\"name\":\"Qwen3.8-LiveTranslate: Names the speaker. Carries the meaning.\",\"description\":\"API Demo\\nSimultaneous interpretation is not only about translating fast — it must also hear clearly and translate accurately. Qwen3.8-LiveTranslate rebuilds real-time simultaneous interpretation with an Interleave architecture, improving faithfulness, fluency, and conciseness across the board, while average lagging (LAAL) drops from 2.8 seconds to 2.3 seconds. We want simultaneous interpretation to convey not only language but also the people and context behind a conversation. Building on support for 60 languages, Qwen3.\",\"keywords\":[\"Open-source\"],\"articleBody\":\" API Demo\\nSimultaneous interpretation is not only about translating fast — it must also hear clearly and translate accurately. Qwen3.8-LiveTranslate rebuilds real-time simultaneous interpretation with an Interleave architecture, improving faithfulness, fluency, and conciseness across the board, while average lagging (LAAL) drops from 2.8 seconds to 2.3 seconds. We want simultaneous interpretation to convey not only language but also the people and context behind a conversation. Building on support for 60 languages, Qwen3.8-LiveTranslate adds three capabilities that bring simultaneous interpretation to a wider range of real-world scenarios: real-time speaker separation, with clear attribution for every sentence and more stable voice cloning; synchronized source-and-translation output on one bilingual screen; and long-context disambiguation that reads the present in light of what came before, making names and terminology more precise.\\nKey Highlights Interleave architecture: audio and text woven together, higher quality and lower latency. This generation recasts simultaneous interpretation as a single audio-text interleaved stream; both the audio already heard and the translation already produced can be cached and reused, so translation quality improves markedly over the previous generation while average lagging (LAAL) drops from 2.8 seconds in the previous generation to 2.3 seconds.\\nReal-time speaker separation: clearer content attribution, more stable voice cloning. When several people speak in turn, the model distinguishes different speakers and what each of them says, and helps the translated speech preserve each speaker’s timbre more accurately and stably.\\nSynchronized source and translation. Bilingual alignment during interpretation supports both instant comprehension and source checking, laying a foundation for follow-on features such as subtitle display, content organization, and retrieval — with broader room for application.\\nLong-context disambiguation: linking history across turns for more precise translation. By drawing on prior text and historical context, the model eases the ambiguity of proper nouns, references, and the like in complex scenarios, keeping expression consistent.\\nModel Architecture Qwen3.8-LiveTranslate adopts a Hybrid-MoE-based Thinker–Talker two-module design, connecting streaming understanding, text output, and speech generation through interleaving. Here, the Thinker arranges video, audio, source text, and translation into a single causal sequence — interleaved in temporal order and produced end to end — so that understanding and translation happen within one sequence; the Talker then combines the translation and the source audio to synthesize the translation into speech that preserves the original speaker’s timbre.\\nPerformance Multi-Speaker Long-Audio On Omnilingua-MSpeaker, a multi-speaker long-audio evaluation set covering 14 language directions, Qwen3.8-LiveTranslate outperforms today’s mainstream real-time interpretation systems across four dimensions: translation faithfulness, fluency, and conciseness, together with the Diarization Error Rate (DER).\\nMultilingual Performance We evaluate real-time interpretation performance across 70 language directions on the public FLEURS audio test set. Qwen3.8-LiveTranslate leads both the previous generation and current mainstream real-time interpretation systems across four dimensions: translation quality, average lagging, speech recognition accuracy, and speech synthesis quality.\\n🎬 See It in Action Using two dialogues from Journey to the West and a set of homophone examples, the demos below showcase speaker attribution and voice cloning, synchronized bilingual output, and how context and visual information help with translation disambiguation.\\nSupported Languages Category Supported languages Input audio \\u0026 output text 60 languages\\nChinese, English, German, Italian, Portuguese, Spanish, Japanese, Korean, French, Russian, Thai, Indonesian, Arabic, Vietnamese, Turkish, Finnish, Polish, Hindi, Dutch, Czech, Urdu, Filipino, Swedish, Danish, Hebrew, Icelandic, Malay, Norwegian, Persian, Afrikaans, Asturian, Azerbaijani, Belarusian, Bengali, Bosnian, Bulgarian, Cantonese, Catalan, Cebuano, Croatian, Estonian, Galician, Gujarati, Greek, Hungarian, Javanese, Kannada, Kazakh, Kyrgyz, Latvian, Macedonian, Malayalam, Marathi, Punjabi, Romanian, Slovak, Slovenian, Swahili, Tajik, Ukrainian Output audio 29 languages\\nChinese, English, German, Italian, Portuguese, Spanish, Japanese, Korean, French, Russian, Thai, Indonesian, Arabic, Vietnamese, Turkish, Finnish, Polish, Hindi, Dutch, Czech, Urdu, Filipino, Swedish, Danish, Hebrew, Icelandic, Malay, Norwegian, Persian Using Qwen3.8-LiveTranslate via DashScope API Below is a complete real-time interpretation client example: it captures microphone audio, streams it to the server, and receives and plays back the translation. This generation’s new speaker separation and synchronized source-and-translation output capabilities are both enabled through session configuration; the server returns the speaker identifier and the source-language text alongside the translation, with no need to call a separate ASR interface.\\nimport os import time import base64 import asyncio import json import websockets import pyaudio import queue import threading import traceback class LiveTranslateClient: \\\"\\\"\\\"Client for the DashScope live-translation service: captures mic audio, sends it to the server, and plays back the translated speech.\\\"\\\"\\\" def __init__(self, api_key: str, workspace_id: str, target_language: str = \\\"en\\\", *, audio_enabled: bool = True): if not api_key: raise ValueError(\\\"API key cannot be empty.\\\") if not workspace_id: raise ValueError(\\\"Workspace ID cannot be empty.\\\") self.api_key = api_key self.workspace_id = workspace_id self.target_language = target_language self.audio_enabled = audio_enabled self.ws = None # URL for the China (Beijing) region; replace {workspace_id} with your Model Studio workspace ID. URLs differ by region. self.api_url = ( f\\\"wss://{workspace_id}.cn-beijing.maas.aliyuncs.com\\\" \\\"/api-ws/v1/realtime?model=qwen3.8-livetranslate-flash-realtime\\\" ) # Audio input parameters (microphone capture) self.input_rate = 16000 self.input_chunk = 1600 self.input_format = pyaudio.paInt16 self.input_channels = 1 # Audio output parameters (local playback) self.output_rate = 24000 self.output_chunk = 2400 self.output_format = pyaudio.paInt16 self.output_channels = 1 # Runtime state and playback resources self.is_connected = False self.audio_player_thread = None self.audio_playback_queue = queue.Queue() self.pyaudio_instance = pyaudio.PyAudio() # Set once the server returns session.finished, so close() can wait for a clean shutdown. self.session_finished_event = asyncio.Event() async def connect(self): \\\"\\\"\\\"Open a WebSocket connection to the translation service.\\\"\\\"\\\" headers = {\\\"Authorization\\\": f\\\"Bearer {self.api_key}\\\"} try: self.ws = await websockets.connect(self.api_url, additional_headers=headers) self.is_connected = True print(f\\\"Successfully connected to server: {self.api_url}\\\") await self.configure_session() except Exception as e: print(f\\\"Connection failed: {e}\\\") self.is_connected = False raise async def configure_session(self): \\\"\\\"\\\"Configure the translation session: target language, audio formats, and optional features.\\\"\\\"\\\" config = { \\\"event_id\\\": f\\\"event_{int(time.time() * 1000)}\\\", \\\"type\\\": \\\"session.update\\\", \\\"session\\\": { # `output_modalities` decides what the server returns: # [\\\"text\\\", \\\"audio\\\"] — both translated text and synthesized speech (recommended) # [\\\"text\\\"] — translated text only \\\"output_modalities\\\": [\\\"text\\\", \\\"audio\\\"] if self.audio_enabled else [\\\"text\\\"], \\\"input_audio_format\\\": \\\"pcm\\\", \\\"output_audio_format\\\": \\\"pcm\\\", # `input_audio_transcription`: enable source-language ASR. # Setting `model` to 'qwen3-asr-flash-realtime' also streams back the source transcript. # \\\"input_audio_transcription\\\": { # \\\"model\\\": \\\"qwen3-asr-flash-realtime\\\", # \\\"language\\\": \\\"zh\\\" # source language; defaults to 'en' # }, \\\"translation\\\": { \\\"language\\\": self.target_language, # `corpus`: register hotwords to boost accuracy on proper nouns and domain-specific terms. # \\\"corpus\\\": { # \\\"phrases\\\": { # \\\"人工智能\\\": \\\"Artificial Intelligence\\\", # \\\"机器学习\\\": \\\"Machine Learning\\\" # } # } } } } print(f\\\"Sending session config: {json.dumps(config, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False)}\\\") await self.ws.send(json.dumps(config)) async def send_audio_chunk(self, audio_data: bytes): \\\"\\\"\\\"Base64-encode an audio chunk and send it to the server.\\\"\\\"\\\" if not self.is_connected: return event = { \\\"event_id\\\": f\\\"event_{int(time.time() * 1000)}\\\", \\\"type\\\": \\\"input_audio_buffer.append\\\", \\\"audio\\\": base64.b64encode(audio_data).decode() } await self.ws.send(json.dumps(event)) async def send_image_frame(self, image_bytes: bytes, *, event_id: str | None = None): \\\"\\\"\\\"Send an image frame to the server as visual context for translation.\\\"\\\"\\\" if not self.is_connected: return if not image_bytes: raise ValueError(\\\"image_bytes cannot be empty\\\") image_b64 = base64.b64encode(image_bytes).decode() event = { \\\"event_id\\\": event_id or f\\\"event_{int(time.time() * 1000)}\\\", \\\"type\\\": \\\"input_image_buffer.append\\\", \\\"image\\\": image_b64, } await self.ws.send(json.dumps(event)) def _audio_player_task(self): \\\"\\\"\\\"Background thread task: drain PCM chunks from the playback queue and write them to the speaker output stream.\\\"\\\"\\\" stream = self.pyaudio_instance.open( format=self.output_format, channels=self.output_channels, rate=self.output_rate, output=True, frames_per_buffer=self.output_chunk, ) try: while self.is_connected or not self.audio_playback_queue.empty(): try: audio_chunk = self.audio_playback_queue.get(timeout=0.1) if audio_chunk is None: # sentinel: stop the playback loop break stream.write(audio_chunk) self.audio_playback_queue.task_done() except queue.Empty: continue finally: stream.stop_stream() stream.close() def start_audio_player(self): \\\"\\\"\\\"Spin up the background audio playback thread (no-op when audio output is disabled).\\\"\\\"\\\" if not self.audio_enabled: return if self.audio_player_thread is None or not self.audio_player_thread.is_alive(): self.audio_player_thread = threading.Thread(target=self._audio_player_task, daemon=True) self.audio_player_thread.start() async def handle_server_messages(self, on_text_received): \\\"\\\"\\\"Continuously receive and dispatch event messages pushed by the server.\\\"\\\"\\\" try: async for message in self.ws: event = json.loads(message) event_type = event.get(\\\"type\\\") if event_type == \\\"response.audio.delta\\\" and self.audio_enabled: audio_b64 = event.get(\\\"delta\\\", \\\"\\\") if audio_b64: audio_data = base64.b64decode(audio_b64) self.audio_playback_queue.put(audio_data) elif event_type == \\\"response.done\\\": print(\\\"\\\\n[INFO] Response complete.\\\") usage = event.get(\\\"response\\\", {}).get(\\\"usage\\\", {}) if usage: print(f\\\"[INFO] Token usage: {json.dumps(usage, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False)}\\\") # Receive source-language ASR results (requires input_audio_transcription.model to be enabled) # elif event_type == \\\"conversation.item.input_audio_transcription.delta\\\": # delta = event.get(\\\"delta\\\", \\\"\\\") # incremental transcript # print(f\\\"[Recognizing] {delta}\\\", end=\\\"\\\", flush=True) # elif event_type == \\\"conversation.item.input_audio_transcription.completed\\\": # transcript = event.get(\\\"transcript\\\", \\\"\\\") # final transcript for an utterance # print(f\\\"[Source] {transcript}\\\") # In voice + text mode, the incremental translation text arrives alongside synthesized audio elif event_type == \\\"response.audio_transcript.delta\\\": on_text_received(event.get(\\\"delta\\\", \\\"\\\")) # In text-only mode, the incremental translation arrives via response.text.delta elif event_type == \\\"response.text.delta\\\": on_text_received(event.get(\\\"delta\\\", \\\"\\\")) # The server acknowledges session.finish and completes the shutdown handshake elif event_type == \\\"session.finished\\\": print(\\\"\\\\n[INFO] Session finished.\\\") self.session_finished_event.set() except websockets.exceptions.ConnectionClosed as e: print(f\\\"[WARNING] Connection closed: {e}\\\") self.is_connected = False except Exception as e: print(f\\\"[ERROR] Unknown error during message handling: {e}\\\") traceback.print_exc() self.is_connected = False async def start_microphone_streaming(self): \\\"\\\"\\\"Continuously capture microphone audio and stream it to the server in real time.\\\"\\\"\\\" stream = self.pyaudio_instance.open( format=self.input_format, channels=self.input_channels, rate=self.input_rate, input=True, frames_per_buffer=self.input_chunk ) print(\\\"Microphone started, please begin speaking...\\\") try: while self.is_connected: audio_chunk = await asyncio.get_event_loop().run_in_executor( None, stream.read, self.input_chunk ) await self.send_audio_chunk(audio_chunk) finally: stream.stop_stream() stream.close() async def close(self): \\\"\\\"\\\"Gracefully close the WebSocket connection and release audio resources.\\\"\\\"\\\" # Ask the server to finish the session, then wait for its session.finished acknowledgement. if self.is_connected and self.ws: finish_event = { \\\"event_id\\\": f\\\"event_{int(time.time() * 1000)}\\\", \\\"type\\\": \\\"session.finish\\\", } await self.ws.send(json.dumps(finish_event)) print(\\\"Sent session.finish, waiting for the server to finish processing...\\\") try: await asyncio.wait_for(self.session_finished_event.wait(), timeout=15) print(\\\"Server finished processing.\\\") except asyncio.TimeoutError: print(\\\"Timed out waiting for session.finished.\\\") self.is_connected = False if self.ws: await self.ws.close() print(\\\"WebSocket connection closed.\\\") if self.audio_player_thread: self.audio_playback_queue.put(None) # signal the playback thread to exit self.audio_player_thread.join(timeout=1) print(\\\"Audio playback thread stopped.\\\") self.pyaudio_instance.terminate() print(\\\"PyAudio instance released.\\\") def print_banner(): print(\\\"=\\\" * 60) print(\\\" Powered by Qwen qwen3.8-livetranslate-flash-realtime\\\") print(\\\"=\\\" * 60 + \\\"\\\\n\\\") def get_user_config(): \\\"\\\"\\\"Collect runtime parameters from the user via CLI: output mode and target language.\\\"\\\"\\\" print(\\\"Select mode:\\\") print(\\\"1. Voice + Text [default] | 2. Text only\\\") mode_choice = input(\\\"Enter option (press Enter for Voice + Text): \\\").strip() audio_enabled = (mode_choice != \\\"2\\\") if audio_enabled: lang_map = { \\\"1\\\": \\\"en\\\", \\\"2\\\": \\\"zh\\\", \\\"3\\\": \\\"ru\\\", \\\"4\\\": \\\"fr\\\", \\\"5\\\": \\\"de\\\", \\\"6\\\": \\\"pt\\\", \\\"7\\\": \\\"es\\\", \\\"8\\\": \\\"it\\\", \\\"9\\\": \\\"ko\\\", \\\"10\\\": \\\"ja\\\", \\\"11\\\": \\\"yue\\\" } print(\\\"Select target translation language (Voice + Text mode):\\\") print(\\\"1. English | 2. Chinese | 3. Russian | 4. French | 5. German | 6. Portuguese | 7. Spanish | 8. Italian | 9. Korean | 10. Japanese | 11. Cantonese\\\") else: lang_map = { \\\"1\\\": \\\"en\\\", \\\"2\\\": \\\"zh\\\", \\\"3\\\": \\\"ru\\\", \\\"4\\\": \\\"fr\\\", \\\"5\\\": \\\"de\\\", \\\"6\\\": \\\"pt\\\", \\\"7\\\": \\\"es\\\", \\\"8\\\": \\\"it\\\", \\\"9\\\": \\\"id\\\", \\\"10\\\": \\\"ko\\\", \\\"11\\\": \\\"ja\\\", \\\"12\\\": \\\"vi\\\", \\\"13\\\": \\\"th\\\", \\\"14\\\": \\\"ar\\\", \\\"15\\\": \\\"yue\\\", \\\"16\\\": \\\"hi\\\", \\\"17\\\": \\\"el\\\", \\\"18\\\": \\\"tr\\\" } print(\\\"Select target translation language (Text only mode):\\\") print(\\\"1. English | 2. Chinese | 3. Russian | 4. French | 5. German | 6. Portuguese | 7. Spanish | 8. Italian | 9. Indonesian | 10. Korean | 11. Japanese | 12. Vietnamese | 13. Thai | 14. Arabic | 15. Cantonese | 16. Hindi | 17. Greek | 18. Turkish\\\") choice = input(\\\"Enter option (default is the first one): \\\").strip() target_language = lang_map.get(choice, next(iter(lang_map.values()))) return target_language, audio_enabled async def main(): \\\"\\\"\\\"Program entry point: connect, configure the session, and drive the live-translation loop.\\\"\\\"\\\" print_banner() api_key = os.environ.get(\\\"DASHSCOPE_API_KEY\\\") if not api_key: print(\\\"[ERROR] Please set the environment variable DASHSCOPE_API_KEY\\\") print(\\\" Example: export DASHSCOPE_API_KEY='your_api_key_here'\\\") return workspace_id = os.environ.get(\\\"DASHSCOPE_WORKSPACE_ID\\\") if not workspace_id: print(\\\"[ERROR] Please set the environment variable DASHSCOPE_WORKSPACE_ID (your Model Studio workspace ID)\\\") print(\\\" Example: export DASHSCOPE_WORKSPACE_ID='your_workspace_id_here'\\\") return target_language, audio_enabled = get_user_config() print(\\\"\\\\nConfiguration complete:\\\") print(f\\\" - Target language: {target_language}\\\") if not audio_enabled: print(\\\" - Output mode: Text only\\\") client = LiveTranslateClient(api_key=api_key, workspace_id=workspace_id, target_language=target_language, audio_enabled=audio_enabled) # Callback fired as translated text arrives — stream it to stdout, character by character def on_translation_text(text): print(text, end=\\\"\\\", flush=True) try: print(\\\"Connecting to the translation service...\\\") await client.connect() # Launch the audio playback thread (only does real work when audio output is enabled) client.start_audio_player() print(\\\"\\\\n\\\" + \\\"-\\\" * 60) print(\\\"Connected! Please speak into the microphone.\\\") print(\\\"The program will translate your speech in real time and play the results. Press Ctrl+C to exit.\\\") print(\\\"-\\\" * 60 + \\\"\\\\n\\\") # Run two coroutines concurrently: server-message handling + microphone audio upload message_handler = asyncio.create_task(client.handle_server_messages(on_translation_text)) tasks = [message_handler] # Microphone capture is the translation input source — required regardless of output mode microphone_streamer = asyncio.create_task(client.start_microphone_streaming()) tasks.append(microphone_streamer) await asyncio.gather(*tasks) except KeyboardInterrupt: print(\\\"\\\\n\\\\nUser interrupted, exiting...\\\") except Exception as e: print(f\\\"\\\\nFatal error occurred: {e}\\\") finally: print(\\\"\\\\nCleaning up resources...\\\") await client.close() print(\\\"Program exited.\\\") if __name__ == \\\"__main__\\\": asyncio.run(main()) Future Directions Simultaneous interpretation is far from its final destination. Guided by “hear it more fully, convey it more faithfully, use it more widely,” we are looking toward the following directions:\\nApproaching the latency limit of simultaneous interpretation: keep compressing end-to-end latency, pushing the gap between “hearing” and “translating” toward its limit. Long-term memory across sessions: carry memory across sessions into the next conversation for the same project and the same group of people, getting better the more it is used. Covering more languages: keep extending coverage to more long-tail languages and regional dialects, so real-time interpretation can serve everyone in the world. Carries the meaning.}, url = {https://qwen.ai/blog?id=qwen3.8-livetranslate}, author = {Qwen Team}, month = {September}, year = {2026} } \",\"wordCount\":\"2151\",\"inLanguage\":\"en\",\"datePublished\":\"2026-09-18T17:30:00+08:00\",\"dateModified\":\"2026-09-18T17:30:00+08:00\",\"author\":{\"@type\":\"Person\",\"name\":\"Qwen Team\"},\"mainEntityOfPage\":{\"@type\":\"WebPage\",\"@id\":\"https://qwenlm.github.io/blog/qwen3.8-livetranslate/\"},\"publisher\":{\"@type\":\"Organization\",\"name\":\"Qwen\",\"logo\":{\"@type\":\"ImageObject\",\"url\":\"https://qwenlm.github.io/favicon.png\"}}}</script></head><body id=top><script>const hasHeaderBg=!1</script><header class=header><div class=nav-container><nav class=nav><div class=logo><a href=/ accesskey=h title=\"Qwen (Alt + H)\"><img src=https://qwenlm.github.io/img/logo.png alt aria-label=logo height=30></a></div><ul id=menu><li><a href=/blog/ title=Blog><span>Blog</span></a></li><li><a href=/publication title=Publication><span>Publication</span></a></li><li><a href=/about title=About><span>About</span></a></li><li><a href=https://chat.qwen.ai title=\"Try Qwen Chat\"><span>Try Qwen Chat</span>&nbsp;<svg fill=\"none\" shape-rendering=\"geometricPrecision\" stroke=\"currentcolor\" stroke-linecap=\"round\" stroke-linejoin=\"round\" stroke-width=\"2.5\" viewBox=\"0 0 24 24\" height=\"12\" width=\"12\"><path d=\"M18 13v6a2 2 0 01-2 2H5a2 2 0 01-2-2V8a2 2 0 012-2h6\"/><path d=\"M15 3h6v6\"/><path d=\"M10 14 21 3\"/></svg></a></li></ul></nav></div></header><div class=hero-container><div class=hero><h1 class=post-title>Qwen3.8-LiveTranslate: Names the speaker. Carries the meaning.</h1><div class=post-meta>&lt;span title='2026-09-18 17:30:00 +0800 CST'>September 18, 2026&lt;/span>&amp;nbsp;·&amp;nbsp;11 min&amp;nbsp;·&amp;nbsp;2151 words&amp;nbsp;·&amp;nbsp;Qwen Team&nbsp;|&nbsp;Translations:<ul class=i18n_list><li><a href=https://qwenlm.github.io/zh/blog/qwen3.8-livetranslate/>简体中文</a></li></ul></div></div></div><main class=main><article class=post-single><div class=post-content><figure><img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen3.8-LiveTranslate/main_v3_en.png alt=\"Qwen3.8-LiveTranslate Main Image\" width=100%></figure><p><a href=https://www.qwencloud.com/models/qwen3.8-livetranslate-flash-realtime class=\"btn external\" target=_blank>API</a>\n<a href=https://omni.qwen.ai/live-translate class=\"btn external\" target=_blank>Demo</a></p><p>Simultaneous interpretation is not only about translating fast — it must also hear clearly and translate accurately. Qwen3.8-LiveTranslate rebuilds real-time simultaneous interpretation with an Interleave architecture, improving faithfulness, fluency, and conciseness across the board, while average lagging (LAAL) drops from 2.8 seconds to 2.3 seconds. We want simultaneous interpretation to convey not only language but also the people and context behind a conversation. Building on support for 60 languages, Qwen3.8-LiveTranslate adds three capabilities that bring simultaneous interpretation to a wider range of real-world scenarios: real-time speaker separation, with clear attribution for every sentence and more stable voice cloning; synchronized source-and-translation output on one bilingual screen; and long-context disambiguation that reads the present in light of what came before, making names and terminology more precise.</p><h3 id=key-highlights>Key Highlights<a hidden class=anchor aria-hidden=true href=#key-highlights>#</a></h3><figure><img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen3.8-LiveTranslate/features-en-loop-2x.gif alt=\"Qwen3.8-LiveTranslate Features Overview\" width=100%></figure><ul><li><p><strong>Interleave architecture: audio and text woven together, higher quality and lower latency.</strong> This generation recasts simultaneous interpretation as a single audio-text interleaved stream; both the audio already heard and the translation already produced can be cached and reused, so translation quality improves markedly over the previous generation while average lagging (LAAL) drops from 2.8 seconds in the previous generation to 2.3 seconds.</p></li><li><p><strong>Real-time speaker separation: clearer content attribution, more stable voice cloning.</strong> When several people speak in turn, the model distinguishes different speakers and what each of them says, and helps the translated speech preserve each speaker&rsquo;s timbre more accurately and stably.</p></li><li><p><strong>Synchronized source and translation.</strong> Bilingual alignment during interpretation supports both instant comprehension and source checking, laying a foundation for follow-on features such as subtitle display, content organization, and retrieval — with broader room for application.</p></li><li><p><strong>Long-context disambiguation: linking history across turns for more precise translation.</strong> By drawing on prior text and historical context, the model eases the ambiguity of proper nouns, references, and the like in complex scenarios, keeping expression consistent.</p></li></ul><h2 id=model-architecture>Model Architecture<a hidden class=anchor aria-hidden=true href=#model-architecture>#</a></h2><p>Qwen3.8-LiveTranslate adopts a Hybrid-MoE-based Thinker–Talker two-module design, connecting streaming understanding, text output, and speech generation through interleaving. Here, the Thinker arranges video, audio, source text, and translation into a single causal sequence — interleaved in temporal order and produced end to end — so that understanding and translation happen within one sequence; the Talker then combines the translation and the source audio to synthesize the translation into speech that preserves the original speaker&rsquo;s timbre.</p><figure><img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen3.8-LiveTranslate/architecture-loop-en-2x.webp alt=\"Qwen3.8-LiveTranslate Model Architecture\" width=100%></figure><h2 id=performance>Performance<a hidden class=anchor aria-hidden=true href=#performance>#</a></h2><h3 id=multi-speaker-long-audio>Multi-Speaker Long-Audio<a hidden class=anchor aria-hidden=true href=#multi-speaker-long-audio>#</a></h3><p>On <a href=https://github.com/QwenLM/Omnilingua-Bench>Omnilingua-MSpeaker</a>, a multi-speaker long-audio evaluation set covering 14 language directions, Qwen3.8-LiveTranslate outperforms today&rsquo;s mainstream real-time interpretation systems across four dimensions: translation faithfulness, fluency, and conciseness, together with the Diarization Error Rate (DER).</p><figure><img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen3.8-LiveTranslate/multispk_en.png alt=\"Multi-Speaker Long-Audio Results\" width=100%></figure><h3 id=multilingual-performance>Multilingual Performance<a hidden class=anchor aria-hidden=true href=#multilingual-performance>#</a></h3><p>We evaluate real-time interpretation performance across 70 language directions on the public FLEURS audio test set. Qwen3.8-LiveTranslate leads both the previous generation and current mainstream real-time interpretation systems across four dimensions: translation quality, average lagging, speech recognition accuracy, and speech synthesis quality.</p><figure><img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen3.8-LiveTranslate/fleurs_en.png alt=\"Multilingual Speech Translation (FLEURS) Results\" width=100%></figure><h2 id=-see-it-in-action>🎬 See It in Action<a hidden class=anchor aria-hidden=true href=#-see-it-in-action>#</a></h2><p>Using two dialogues from <em>Journey to the West</em> and a set of homophone examples, the demos below showcase speaker attribution and voice cloning, synchronized bilingual output, and how context and visual information help with translation disambiguation.</p><figure><video controls src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen3.8-LiveTranslate/cases/demo_speaker_clone_v3_en.mp4></video></figure><figure><video controls src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen3.8-LiveTranslate/cases/demo_long_context_v3_en.mp4></video></figure><figure><video controls src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen3.8-LiveTranslate/cases/demo_visual_disambiguation_v3_en.mp4></video></figure><h2 id=supported-languages>Supported Languages<a hidden class=anchor aria-hidden=true href=#supported-languages>#</a></h2><div style=\"font-family:-apple-system,BlinkMacSystemFont,segoe ui,Roboto,sans-serif;color:#1a1a2e;max-width:1250px;margin:0 auto;padding:16px 0\"><table style=width:100%;border-collapse:collapse;font-size:1rem><thead><tr><th style=\"width:220px;padding:10px 12px;text-align:left;font-weight:600;border-bottom:2px solid #7c3aed;color:#7c3aed\">Category</th><th style=\"padding:10px 12px;text-align:left;font-weight:500;border-bottom:2px solid #7c3aed;color:#7c3aed\">Supported languages</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td rgba(128,128,128,.15)\"><b>60 languages</b><br>Chinese, English, German, Italian, Portuguese, Spanish, Japanese, Korean, French, Russian, Thai, Indonesian, Arabic, Vietnamese, Turkish, Finnish, Polish, Hindi, Dutch, Czech, Urdu, Filipino, Swedish, Danish, Hebrew, Icelandic, Malay, Norwegian, Persian, Afrikaans, Asturian, Azerbaijani, Belarusian, Bengali, Bosnian, Bulgarian, Cantonese, Catalan, Cebuano, Croatian, Estonian, Galician, Gujarati, Greek, Hungarian, Javanese, Kannada, Kazakh, Kyrgyz, Latvian, Macedonian, Malayalam, Marathi, Punjabi, Romanian, Slovak, Slovenian, Swahili, Polish, Hindi, Dutch, Czech, Urdu, Filipino, Swedish, Danish, Hebrew, Icelandic, Malay, Norwegian, Persian</td></tr></tbody></table></div><h2 id=using-qwen38-livetranslate-via-dashscope-api>Using Qwen3.8-LiveTranslate via DashScope API<a hidden class=anchor aria-hidden=true href=#using-qwen38-livetranslate-via-dashscope-api>#</a></h2><p>Below is a complete real-time interpretation client example: it captures microphone audio, streams it to the server, and receives and plays back the translation. This generation&rsquo;s new <strong>speaker separation</strong> and <strong>synchronized source-and-translation output</strong> capabilities are both enabled through session configuration; the server returns the speaker identifier and the source-language text alongside the translation, with no need to call a separate ASR interface.</p><div class=highlight><pre tabindex=0 class=chroma><code class=language-python data-lang=python><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=kn>import</span> <span class=nn>os</span>\n</span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=kn>import</span> <span class=nn>time</span>\n</span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=kn>import</span> <span class=nn>base64</span>\n</span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span plays back the translated speech.&#34;&#34;&#34;</span>\n</span></span><span class=line><span class=cl>\n</span></span><span class=line><span class=cl>    <span class=k>def</span> <span class=fm>__init__</span><span class=p>(</span><span class=bp>self</span><span class=p>,</span> <span class=n>api_key</span><span class=p>:</span> <span class=nb>str</span><span class=p>,</span> <span <span class=n>api_key</span><span class=p>:</span>\n</span></span><span class=line><span class=cl>            <span class=k>raise</span> <span class=ne>ValueError</span><span class=p>(</span><span class=s2>&#34;API key cannot be empty.&#34;</span><span class=p>)</span>\n</span></span><span class=line><span class=cl>        <span class=k>if</span> <span class=ow>not</span> <span class=p>(</span><span class=n>main</span><span class=p>())</span>\n</span></span></code></pre></div><h2 id=future-directions>Future Directions<a hidden class=anchor aria-hidden=true href=#future-directions>#</a></h2><p>Simultaneous interpretation is far from its final destination. Guided by &ldquo;hear it more fully, convey it more faithfully, use it more widely,&rdquo; we are looking toward the following directions:</p><ul><li><strong>Approaching the latency limit of simultaneous interpretation</strong>: keep compressing end-to-end latency, pushing the gap between &ldquo;hearing&rdquo; and &ldquo;translating&rdquo; toward its limit.</li><li><strong>Long-term memory across sessions</strong>: carry memory across sessions into the next conversation for the same project and the same group of people, getting better the more it is used.</li><li><strong>Covering more languages</strong>: keep extending coverage to more long-tail languages and regional dialects, so real-time interpretation can serve everyone in the world.</li></ul><h2 id=citation>Citation<a hidden class=anchor aria-hidden=true href=#citation>#</a></h2><p>Feel free to cite the following article if you find Qwen3.8-LiveTranslate helpful:</p><div class=highlight><pre tabindex=0 class=chroma><code class=language-bibtex data-lang=bibtex><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=nc>@misc</span><span class=p>{</span><span class=nl>qwen38livetranslateblog</span><span class=p>,</span>\n</span></span><span class=line><span class=cl>    <span class=na>title</span> <span class=p>=</span> <span class=s>{Qwen3.8-LiveTranslate: Names the speaker. Qwen-Image-2.1 unifies text-to-image generation and image editing in a single model, with just 7B parameters in its visual generation component and native support for generating and editing transparent images.\\nThis update introduces four key improvements:\\nCompact and efficient: A lightweight architecture and inference optimizations balance image quality and computational cost.\",\"keywords\":[],\"articleBody\":\" GITHUB HUGGING FACE MODELSCOPE\\nWe are excited to open-source Qwen-Image-2.1, an image model in the Qwen family that balances generation quality, inference efficiency, and cost. Qwen-Image-2.1 unifies text-to-image generation and image editing in a single model, with just 7B parameters in its visual generation component and native support for generating and editing transparent images.\\nThis update introduces four key improvements:\\nCompact and efficient: A lightweight architecture and inference optimizations balance image quality and computational cost. Native transparency, unified creation and editing: Generate regular or transparent images from prompts, edit transparent layers, and extract subjects from photos. Versatile editing: Use up to 10 reference images, specify local edits, preserve people and products, and handle a wide range of tasks. Realistic textures and refined aesthetics: Improved typography, portrait lighting, and fine details make generated images more visually compelling. Compact and Efficient Qwen-Image-2.1 uses a lightweight architecture. Its visual generation component contains 32 Single-Stream DiT layers and 7B parameters. Despite its compact size, the model delivers strong image generation quality. The Qwen-Image-Bench comparison below shows its performance alongside other open-source and closed-source models.\\nQwen-Image-Bench evaluation comparison\\nInference efficiency is another focus, particularly when editing with multiple input images. This optimization comes from a mixed-granularity attention architecture. Text, including the system prefix and editing instructions, uses a token-level causal mask, while image generation uses a chunk-level mask. KV cache reuse allows input images and editing instructions to serve as static context, computed and cached in the first step to improve inference efficiency and reduce memory usage.\\nQwen-Image-2.1 mixed-granularity attention architecture\\nNative Transparency, Unified Creation and Editing Native transparency is a major addition in this release. In December 2025, we introduced Qwen-Image-Layered as a dedicated model supporting transparent image generation. Qwen-Image-2.1 now integrates this capability into a unified model, using the prompt to determine whether to output a regular image or an image with a transparency channel.\\nHere are examples of transparent images generated directly from text:\\nBeyond individual subjects, the model can generate more complex transparent images composed of multiple elements, opening up further possibilities for design and asset creation.\\nBy unifying generation and editing, Qwen-Image-2.1 also supports editing transparent images directly. For example, it can change a subject’s expression while retaining the transparent background. The input is on the left and the edited result on the right.\\nText within a transparent layer can also be edited. In the following example, “BLOOM” is replaced with “Qwen-Image”.\\nThis capability extends to real photographs. Given an RGB image, the model can extract the desired subject as an RGBA layer with transparency, making it easier to reuse elements in subsequent design and composition work.\\nVersatile Editing Qwen-Image-2.1 improves editing in four areas: multiple reference images, local editing, fidelity preservation, and task coverage.\\nMultiple References: Up to 10 Input Images Qwen-Image-2.1 supports up to 10 reference images, combining multiple subjects and assets into a coherent composition. In the group portrait example below, six individual portraits on the left are brought together in one photograph.\\nA group photograph generated from six portrait references\\nFor virtual try-on, five inputs—a model, clothing, shoes, a bag, and a hat—can be combined into a complete outfit.\\nA complete outfit generated from five reference images\\nFor interior design, the model can use 10 images of furnishings to generate a complete room arrangement.\\nAn interior generated from ten furnishing references\\nLocal Editing: Flexible Region Selection Qwen-Image-2.1 supports circles, painted annotations, and separate masks to specify where an edit should take place.\\nThe first example uses circles of different colors to identify three regions at once: “Remove the metal watch in the blue circle, change the hair in the red circle to black, and replace the area in the green circle with gray short-sleeved linen pajamas.” The annotated input is on the left, and the result is on the right.\\nPainted annotations provide another way to identify a region. In this example, the model adds a diver to the area marked in white on the right side of the input image.\\nCircles and painted annotations obscure some of the original content. To preserve the complete input, Qwen-Image-2.1 also accepts the original image and a separate mask as two inputs. The following example asks the model to generate a cowboy on horseback, using these two images:\\nThe model edits the region specified by the mask, producing the following result:\\nMask-guided editing result: a cowboy on horseback\\nLocal editing can also support sequential creation. By making successive changes while preserving the rest of the scene, edited images can be assembled into simple animations, as illustrated by the capybara Calabash Brothers example below.\\nCapybara Calabash Brothers: animation through successive local edits Fidelity: Preserving People and Products Fidelity improvements focus on portrait identity and product consistency. For portraits, the model better preserves facial features so that a person’s identity remains more consistent across edits. Each pair below shows the input on the left and the edited result on the right.\\nFor product editing, the model aims to preserve text, textures, and shape, keeping the product’s defining features consistent in a new image.\\nTask Coverage: Panoramas, Infographics, and Storyboards Qwen-Image-2.1 supports a broad range of editing tasks, including panorama, infographic, and storyboard generation, balancing capabilities across different applications.\\nFor example, starting from this selfie:\\nInput selfie for panorama generation\\nThe model generates the following panorama:\\nPanorama generated from the selfie\\nThe panorama can then be explored from different viewing directions in a visualization tool.\\nPanorama visualization demonstration For infographic generation, a model photograph can be expanded into a detailed, information-rich composition.\\nInput model photograph for infographic generation\\nComplex infographic generated from the model photograph\\nThe model can also turn a three-view character reference into a complete storyboard, providing material for storytelling and visual narratives.\\nThree-view character reference for storyboard generation\\nComplete storyboard generated from the character reference\\nRealistic Textures and Refined Aesthetics Qwen-Image-2.1 further improves visual quality, with particular attention to typography and portraits. Text rendering considers not only the content itself, but also type styles, layout, and their relationship to the overall composition. The following examples showcase text rendering across different settings.\\nText rendering example one\\nText rendering example two\\nText rendering example three\\nText rendering example four\\nFor portraits, improved lighting and fine details give generated images a more realistic appearance.\\nPortrait lighting and detail example one\\nPortrait lighting and detail example two\\nConclusion With a compact 7B visual generation component, Qwen-Image-2.1 brings image generation, transparency, and a wide range of editing capabilities together in one model. Faster inference, support for up to 10 reference images, flexible local editing, and improved fidelity for people and products make it a practical tool for design, content creation, e-commerce, and visual storytelling.\\nWe hope you enjoy creating with Qwen-Image-2.1!\\n\",\"wordCount\":\"1117\",\"inLanguage\":\"en\",\"datePublished\":\"2026-09-20T20:00:00+08:00\",\"dateModified\":\"2026-09-20T20:00:00+08:00\",\"author\":{\"@type\":\"Person\",\"name\":\"Qwen class=hero-container><div class=hero><h1 class=post-title>Qwen-Image-2.1: Compact, Efficient, and Unified Image Creation</h1><div class=post-meta>&lt;span title='2026-09-20 20:00:00 +0800 CST'>September 20, 2026&lt;/span>&amp;nbsp;·&amp;nbsp;6 min&amp;nbsp;·&amp;nbsp;1117 words&amp;nbsp;·&amp;nbsp;Qwen Team&nbsp;|&nbsp;Translations:<ul class=i18n_list><li><a href=https://qwenlm.github.io/zh/blog/qwen-image-2.1/>简体中文</a></li></ul></div></div></div><main class=main><article class=post-single><div class=post-content><figure><img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/banner_en.png alt=\"Qwen-Image-2.1 banner\" width=100%></figure><p><a href=https://github.com/QwenLM/Qwen-Image-2.1 class=\"btn external\" target=_blank>GITHUB</a>\n<a href=https://huggingface.co/Qwen/Qwen-Image-2.1 class=\"btn external\" target=_blank>HUGGING FACE</a>\n<a href=https://www.modelscope.cn/models/Qwen/Qwen-Image-2.1 class=\"btn external\" target=_blank>MODELSCOPE</a></p><p>We are excited to open-source <strong>Qwen-Image-2.1</strong>, an image model in the Qwen family that balances generation quality, inference efficiency, and cost. Qwen-Image-2.1 unifies text-to-image generation and image editing in a single model, with just <strong>7B parameters in its visual generation component</strong> and native support for generating and editing transparent images.</p><p>This update introduces four key improvements:</p><ul><li><strong>Compact and efficient</strong>: A lightweight architecture and inference optimizations balance image quality and computational cost.</li><li><strong>Native transparency, unified creation and editing</strong>: Generate regular or transparent images from prompts, edit transparent layers, and extract subjects from photos.</li><li><strong>Versatile editing</strong>: Use up to <strong>10 reference images</strong>, specify local edits, preserve people and products, and handle a wide range of tasks.</li><li><strong>Realistic textures and refined aesthetics</strong>: Improved typography, portrait lighting, and fine details make generated images more visually compelling.</li></ul><h2 id=compact-and-efficient>Compact and Efficient<a hidden class=anchor aria-hidden=true href=#compact-and-efficient>#</a></h2><p>Qwen-Image-2.1 uses a lightweight architecture. Its visual generation component contains <strong>32 Single-Stream DiT layers and 7B parameters</strong>. Despite its compact size, the model delivers strong image generation quality. The Qwen-Image-Bench comparison below shows its performance alongside other open-source and closed-source models.</p><figure><img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-01.png alt=\"Qwen-Image-Bench evaluation comparison\" width=100%><figcaption><p>Qwen-Image-Bench evaluation comparison</p></figcaption></figure><p>Inference efficiency is another focus, particularly when editing with multiple input images. This optimization comes from a <strong>mixed-granularity attention architecture</strong>. Text, including the system prefix and editing instructions, uses a token-level causal mask, while image generation uses a chunk-level mask. <strong>KV cache reuse</strong> allows input images and editing instructions to serve as static context, computed and cached in the first step to improve inference efficiency and reduce memory usage.</p><figure><img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-03.png alt=\"Qwen-Image-2.1 mixed-granularity attention architecture\" width=100%><figcaption><p>Qwen-Image-2.1 mixed-granularity attention architecture</p></figcaption></figure><h2 id=native-transparency-unified-creation-and-editing>Native Transparency, Unified Creation and Editing<a hidden class=anchor aria-hidden=true href=#native-transparency-unified-creation-and-editing>#</a></h2><p>Native transparency is a major addition in this release. In December 2025, we introduced Qwen-Image-Layered as a dedicated model supporting transparent image generation. Qwen-Image-2.1 now integrates this capability into a unified model, using the prompt to determine whether to output a regular image or an image with a transparency channel.</p><p>Here are examples of transparent images generated directly from text:</p><div style=display:flex;flex-wrap:wrap;gap:16px;align-items:center><img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-04.png style=\"flex:1 1 180px;min-width:0;width:33.33%;object-fit:contain\" loading=lazy alt=\"Transparent images generated from text (1)\">\n<img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-05.png style=\"flex:1 1 180px;min-width:0;width:33.33%;object-fit:contain\" loading=lazy alt=\"Transparent images generated from text (2)\">\n<img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-06.png style=\"flex:1 1 180px;min-width:0;width:33.33%;object-fit:contain\" loading=lazy alt=\"Transparent images generated from text (3)\"></div><p>Beyond individual subjects, the model can generate more complex transparent images composed of multiple elements, opening up further possibilities for design and asset creation.</p><div style=display:flex;flex-wrap:wrap;gap:16px;align-items:center><img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-07.png style=\"flex:1 1 180px;min-width:0;width:50%;object-fit:contain\" loading=lazy alt=\"Transparent compositions with multiple elements (1)\">\n<img src=\"https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-08.png?v=cac12076c2cd\" style=\"flex:1 1 180px;min-width:0;width:50%;object-fit:contain\" loading=lazy alt=\"Transparent compositions with multiple elements (2)\"></div><p>By unifying generation and editing, Qwen-Image-2.1 also supports editing transparent images directly. For example, it can change a subject&rsquo;s expression while retaining the transparent background. The input is on the left and the edited result on the right.</p><div style=display:flex;flex-wrap:wrap;gap:16px;align-items:center><img src=\"https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-09.png?v=ee4de1b5241d\" style=\"flex:1 1 180px;min-width:0;width:50%;object-fit:contain\" loading=lazy alt=\"Expression editing on a transparent image: input and result (1)\">\n<img src=\"https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-10.png?v=611bf03abb39\" style=\"flex:1 1 180px;min-width:0;width:50%;object-fit:contain\" loading=lazy alt=\"Expression editing on a transparent image: input and result (2)\"></div><p>Text within a transparent layer can also be edited. In the following example, “BLOOM” is replaced with “Qwen-Image”.</p><div style=display:flex;flex-wrap:wrap;gap:16px;align-items:center><img src=\"https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-11.png?v=1f2803db504a\" style=\"flex:1 1 180px;min-width:0;width:50%;object-fit:contain\" loading=lazy alt=\"Transparent text editing: replacing BLOOM with Qwen-Image (1)\">\n<img src=\"https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-12.png?v=9d0fa2b0e13a\" style=\"flex:1 1 180px;min-width:0;width:50%;object-fit:contain\" loading=lazy alt=\"Transparent text editing: replacing BLOOM with Qwen-Image (2)\"></div><p>This capability extends to real photographs. Given an RGB image, the model can extract the desired subject as an RGBA layer with transparency, making it easier to reuse elements in subsequent design and composition work.</p><div style=display:flex;flex-wrap:wrap;gap:16px;align-items:center><img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-13.png style=\"flex:1 1 180px;min-width:0;width:50%;object-fit:contain\" loading=lazy alt=\"Extracting a transparent RGBA layer from an RGB photograph (1)\">\n<img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-14.png style=\"flex:1 1 180px;min-width:0;width:50%;object-fit:contain\" loading=lazy alt=\"Extracting a transparent RGBA layer from an RGB photograph (2)\"></div><h2 id=versatile-editing>Versatile Editing<a hidden class=anchor aria-hidden=true href=#versatile-editing>#</a></h2><p>Qwen-Image-2.1 improves editing in four areas: multiple reference images, local editing, fidelity preservation, and task coverage.</p><h3 id=multiple-references-up-to-10-input-images>Multiple References: Up to 10 Input Images<a hidden class=anchor aria-hidden=true href=#multiple-references-up-to-10-input-images>#</a></h3><p>Qwen-Image-2.1 supports <strong>up to 10 reference images</strong>, combining multiple subjects and assets into a coherent composition. In the group portrait example below, six individual portraits on the left are brought together in one photograph.</p><figure><img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-15.png alt=\"A group photograph generated from six portrait references\" width=100%><figcaption><p>A group photograph generated from six portrait references</p></figcaption></figure><p>For virtual try-on, five inputs—a model, clothing, shoes, a bag, and a hat—can be combined into a complete outfit.</p><figure><img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-16.png alt=\"A complete outfit generated from five reference images\" width=100%><figcaption><p>A complete outfit generated from five reference images</p></figcaption></figure><p>For interior design, the model can use 10 images of furnishings to generate a complete room arrangement.</p><figure><img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-17.png alt=\"An interior generated from ten furnishing references\" width=100%><figcaption><p>An interior generated from ten furnishing references</p></figcaption></figure><h3 id=local-editing-flexible-region-selection>Local Editing: Flexible Region Selection<a hidden class=anchor aria-hidden=true href=#local-editing-flexible-region-selection>#</a></h3><p>Qwen-Image-2.1 supports circles, painted annotations, and separate masks to specify where an edit should take place.</p><p>The first example uses circles of different colors to identify three regions at once: “Remove the metal watch in the blue circle, change the hair in the red circle to black, and replace the area in the green circle with gray short-sleeved linen pajamas.” The annotated input is on the left, and the result is on the right.</p><div style=display:flex;flex-wrap:wrap;gap:16px;align-items:center><img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-18.png style=\"flex:1 1 180px;min-width:0;width:50%;object-fit:contain\" loading=lazy alt=\"Circle-guided editing of a watch, hair, and clothing (1)\">\n<img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-19.png style=\"flex:1 1 180px;min-width:0;width:50%;object-fit:contain\" loading=lazy alt=\"Circle-guided editing of a watch, hair, and clothing (2)\"></div><p>Painted annotations provide another way to identify a region. In this example, the model adds a diver to the area marked in white on the right side of the input image.</p><div style=display:flex;flex-wrap:wrap;gap:16px;align-items:center><img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-20.png style=\"flex:1 1 180px;min-width:0;width:50%;object-fit:contain\" loading=lazy alt=\"Paint-guided editing: adding a diver to the marked region (1)\">\n<img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-21.png style=\"flex:1 1 180px;min-width:0;width:50%;object-fit:contain\" loading=lazy alt=\"Paint-guided editing: adding a diver to the marked region (2)\"></div><p>Circles and painted annotations obscure some of the original content. To preserve the complete input, Qwen-Image-2.1 also accepts <strong>the original image and a separate mask as two inputs</strong>. The following example asks the model to generate a cowboy on horseback, using these two images:</p><div style=display:flex;flex-wrap:wrap;gap:16px;align-items:center><img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-22.png style=\"flex:1 1 180px;min-width:0;width:50%;object-fit:contain\" loading=lazy alt=\"Local editing inputs: original image and separate mask (1)\">\n<img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-23.png style=\"flex:1 1 180px;min-width:0;width:50%;object-fit:contain\" loading=lazy alt=\"Local editing inputs: original image and separate mask (2)\"></div><p>The model edits the region specified by the mask, producing the following result:</p><figure><img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-24.png alt=\"Mask-guided editing result: a cowboy on horseback\" width=100%><figcaption><p>Mask-guided editing result: a cowboy on horseback</p></figcaption></figure><p>Local editing can also support sequential creation. By making successive changes while preserving the rest of the scene, edited images can be assembled into simple animations, as illustrated by the capybara Calabash Brothers example below.</p><video controls loop muted playsinline preload=metadata style=width:100%;height:auto aria-label=\"Capybara Calabash Brothers: animation through successive local edits\">\n<source src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/videos/hulu_final_48f_6fps.mp4 type=video/mp4><a href=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/videos/hulu_final_48f_6fps.mp4>Capybara Calabash Brothers: animation through successive local edits</a></video><h3 id=fidelity-preserving-people-and-products>Fidelity: Preserving People and Products<a hidden class=anchor aria-hidden=true href=#fidelity-preserving-people-and-products>#</a></h3><p>Fidelity improvements focus on <strong>portrait identity</strong> and <strong>product consistency</strong>. For portraits, the model better preserves facial features so that a person&rsquo;s identity remains more consistent across edits. Each pair below shows the input on the left and the edited result on the right.</p><div style=display:flex;flex-wrap:wrap;gap:16px;align-items:center><img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-25.png style=\"flex:1 1 180px;min-width:0;width:50%;object-fit:contain\" loading=lazy alt=\"Portrait fidelity example one: input and result (1)\">\n<img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-26.png style=\"flex:1 1 180px;min-width:0;width:50%;object-fit:contain\" loading=lazy alt=\"Portrait fidelity example one: input and result (2)\"></div><div style=display:flex;flex-wrap:wrap;gap:16px;align-items:center><img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-27.png style=\"flex:1 1 180px;min-width:0;width:50%;object-fit:contain\" loading=lazy alt=\"Portrait fidelity example two: input and result (1)\">\n<img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-28.png style=\"flex:1 1 180px;min-width:0;width:50%;object-fit:contain\" loading=lazy alt=\"Portrait fidelity example two: input and result (2)\"></div><div style=display:flex;flex-wrap:wrap;gap:16px;align-items:center><img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-29.png style=\"flex:1 1 180px;min-width:0;width:50%;object-fit:contain\" loading=lazy alt=\"Portrait fidelity example three: input and result (1)\">\n<img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-30.png style=\"flex:1 1 180px;min-width:0;width:50%;object-fit:contain\" loading=lazy alt=\"Portrait fidelity example three: input and result (2)\"></div><p>For product editing, the model aims to preserve text, textures, and shape, keeping the product&rsquo;s defining features consistent in a new image.</p><div style=display:flex;flex-wrap:wrap;gap:16px;align-items:center><img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-31.png style=\"flex:1 1 180px;min-width:0;width:50%;object-fit:contain\" loading=lazy alt=\"Product fidelity example one: input and result (1)\">\n<img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-32.png style=\"flex:1 1 180px;min-width:0;width:50%;object-fit:contain\" loading=lazy alt=\"Product fidelity example one: input and result (2)\"></div><div style=display:flex;flex-wrap:wrap;gap:16px;align-items:center><img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-33.png style=\"flex:1 1 180px;min-width:0;width:50%;object-fit:contain\" loading=lazy alt=\"Product fidelity example two: input and result (1)\">\n<img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-34.png style=\"flex:1 1 180px;min-width:0;width:50%;object-fit:contain\" loading=lazy alt=\"Product fidelity example two: input and result (2)\"></div><div style=display:flex;flex-wrap:wrap;gap:16px;align-items:center><img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-35.png style=\"flex:1 1 180px;min-width:0;width:50%;object-fit:contain\" loading=lazy alt=\"Product fidelity example three: input and result (1)\">\n<img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-36.png style=\"flex:1 1 180px;min-width:0;width:50%;object-fit:contain\" loading=lazy alt=\"Product fidelity example three: input and result (2)\"></div><h3 id=task-coverage-panoramas-infographics-and-storyboards>Task Coverage: Panoramas, Infographics, and Storyboards<a hidden class=anchor aria-hidden=true href=#task-coverage-panoramas-infographics-and-storyboards>#</a></h3><p>Qwen-Image-2.1 supports a broad range of editing tasks, including panorama, infographic, and storyboard generation, balancing capabilities across different applications.</p><p>For example, starting from this selfie:</p><figure><img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-37.png alt=\"Input selfie for panorama generation\" width=100%><figcaption><p>Input selfie for panorama generation</p></figcaption></figure><p>The model generates the following panorama:</p><figure><img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-38.png alt=\"Panorama generated from the selfie\" width=100%><figcaption><p>Panorama generated from the selfie</p></figcaption></figure><p>The panorama can then be explored from different viewing directions in a visualization tool.</p><video controls loop muted playsinline preload=metadata style=width:100%;height:auto aria-label=\"Panorama visualization demonstration\">\n<source src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/videos/pano_video2_10s_noaudio.mp4 type=video/mp4><a href=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/videos/pano_video2_10s_noaudio.mp4>Panorama visualization demonstration</a></video><p>For infographic generation, a model photograph can be expanded into a detailed, information-rich composition.</p><figure><img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-39.png alt=\"Input model photograph for infographic generation\" width=100%><figcaption><p>Input model photograph for infographic generation</p></figcaption></figure><figure><img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-40.png alt=\"Complex infographic generated from the model photograph\" width=100%><figcaption><p>Complex infographic generated from the model photograph</p></figcaption></figure><p>The model can also turn a three-view character reference into a complete storyboard, providing material for storytelling and visual narratives.</p><figure><img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-41.png alt=\"Three-view character reference for storyboard generation\" width=100%><figcaption><p>Three-view character reference for storyboard generation</p></figcaption></figure><figure><img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-42.png alt=\"Complete storyboard generated from the character reference\" width=100%><figcaption><p>Complete storyboard generated from the character reference</p></figcaption></figure><h2 id=realistic-textures-and-refined-aesthetics>Realistic Textures and Refined Aesthetics<a hidden class=anchor aria-hidden=true href=#realistic-textures-and-refined-aesthetics>#</a></h2><p>Qwen-Image-2.1 further improves visual quality, with particular attention to typography and portraits. Text rendering considers not only the content itself, but also type styles, layout, and their relationship to the overall composition. The following examples showcase text rendering across different settings.</p><figure><img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-43.png alt=\"Text rendering example one\" width=100%><figcaption><p>Text rendering example one</p></figcaption></figure><figure><img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-44.png alt=\"Text rendering example two\" width=100%><figcaption><p>Text rendering example two</p></figcaption></figure><figure><img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-45.png alt=\"Text rendering example three\" width=100%><figcaption><p>Text rendering example three</p></figcaption></figure><figure><img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-46.png alt=\"Text rendering example four\" width=100%><figcaption><p>Text rendering example four</p></figcaption></figure><p>For portraits, improved lighting and fine details give generated images a more realistic appearance.</p><figure><img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-47.png alt=\"Portrait lighting and detail example one\" width=100%><figcaption><p>Portrait lighting and detail example one</p></figcaption></figure><figure><img src=https://qianwen-res.oss-accelerate.aliyuncs.com/Qwen-Image/image2.1/images/example-48.png alt=\"Portrait lighting and detail example two\" width=100%><figcaption><p>Portrait lighting and detail example two</p></figcaption></figure><h2 id=conclusion>Conclusion<a hidden class=anchor aria-hidden=true href=#conclusion>#</a></h2><p>With a compact 7B visual generation component, Qwen-Image-2.1 brings image generation, transparency, and a wide range of editing capabilities together in one model. Faster inference, support for up to 10 reference images, flexible local editing, and improved fidelity for people and products make it a practical tool for design, content creation, e-commerce, and visual storytelling.</p><p>We hope you enjoy creating with Qwen-Image-2.1!</p></div></article></main><footer class=footer><span>&copy; 2026 <a
